--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Output: animation, held props, VFX, root.userData.tick. Held props
attach to hand sockets defined in 19.2. Animations are FK chains or
simple keyframe-driven values on userData.sculptRuntime.animations.
  userData.sculptRuntime.passes.interaction = {
    tickFn: <boolean, true if userData.tick is set>,
    animationCount: <number>,
    heldProps: [{ propName, socketName }, ...],
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
19.8 — optimization
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Output: castShadow / receiveShadow on every mesh, geometry merge where
the renderer supports it, draw-call reduction.
  userData.sculptRuntime.passes.optimization = {
    shadowedMeshes: <count>,
    drawCalls: <estimated count>,
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
19.9 — All-pass envelope
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  userData.sculptRuntime.passesComplete = <boolean>
  userData.sculptRuntime.passesReviewed = {
    blockout:    { score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
    structural:  { score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
    form:        { score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
    material:    { score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
    surface:     { score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
    lighting:    { score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
    interaction: { score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
    optimization:{ score: <0..1>, notes: '...' },
  }
You MUST populate this with your own self-score per pass BEFORE
output. The renderer's Fidelity tab reads it and shows a per-pass bar
chart. A pass with score < 0.6 is flagged.

================================================================================
PART 20 — DETAIL INVENTORY GATE  (img2threejs alignment — added v1.2, 2026-07-31)
================================================================================
Before PART 19 starts, you MUST enumerate every identity-defining
feature of the subject as a flat list. This is img2threejs's
detailInventory — a structured list the build passes check against and
the review gate scores. Without it you are trusted to "remember"
identity features from prose alone, which is the main cause of
"almost right but missed a stripe" failures.

Mandatory shape:
  userData.sculptRuntime.detailInventory = [
    {
      id: 'face.leftEye',         // stable string id, dotted.path style
      region: 'face',             // body region or hard-surface area
      kind: 'feature',            // 'feature' | 'panel' | 'socket' |
                                  // 'decal' | 'prop' | 'landmark'
      description: 'amber iris, ~0.04 radius, slight inner-corner shadow',
      priority: 'high',           // 'high' | 'medium' | 'low'
      reviewThreshold: 0.85,      // 0..1, must clear in review
    },
    ...
  ]

Rules:
  - List MUST be non-empty. A factory with an empty detailInventory is
    rejected by the validator.
  - High-priority items are identity-defining (face features, weapon
    silhouettes, vehicle proportions). The Tier 1 gate checks that
    every high-priority item has at least one Mesh assigned in PART 19.
  - reviewThreshold is the per-item score your own post-render fidelity
    self-check (PART 23) must clear. Default 0.85 for high-priority,
    0.7 for medium, 0.5 for low.
  - Items are sorted by priority then by id for stable diffing across
    regenerations.
  - The validator's coverage check is:
      for each high-priority item with kind in {feature, panel, decal,
      landmark}:
        assert item has at least one matching Mesh in the userData
        tree (matched by .name startsWith item.id with '.' -> '/')
  - Image-fork gets this from PART 17 measurement; text-fork gets it
    from PART 20 semantic priors (this PART itself, on the text path).

================================================================================
PART 21 — LANDMARK PIXEL MEASUREMENT  (img2threejs alignment — added v1.2, 2026-07-31)
================================================================================
You MUST also record the pixel coordinates (normalized 0..1, origin
top-left) of every landmark you use for proportion / pose / feature
placement. This is the same role img2threejs's extract_landmarks.py
plays, and it plugs directly into the renderer's existing compare-
overlay and Fidelity tab (adding landmarks unlocks a real IoU score
without changing the renderer pipeline).

Shape:
  userData.sculptRuntime.landmarks = {
    // Coordinates are (u, v) in [0,1] x [0,1], origin top-left.
    // v=0 is the top of the image, v=1 is the bottom.
    // For 3D placement, multiply by the image's measured bounding
    // box (PART 17.2) to get world-space anchor positions.
    'face.leftEyeCenter':   [0.42, 0.31],
    'face.rightEyeCenter':  [0.58, 0.31],
    'face.noseTip':         [0.50, 0.38],
    'face.chin':            [0.50, 0.62],
    // For hard-surface subjects, use anchor landmarks instead:
    'weapon.barrelTip':     [0.85, 0.45],
    'weapon.triggerGuard':  [0.40, 0.55],
    'weapon.magWell':       [0.30, 0.60],
  }

Rules:
  - Every entry in detailInventory (PART 20) with kind='landmark' MUST
    have a matching key in userData.sculptRuntime.landmarks.
  - The renderer computes IoU between the rendered silhouette and a
    "landmark-projected" silhouette (the convex hull of all landmark
    positions) and reports it in the Fidelity tab as landmarkIou.
  - The compare-overlay draws the landmark positions as small numbered
    markers over the reference image when the user toggles "Show
    landmarks."
================================================================================
PART 22 — SUBJECT ROUTING  (img2threejs alignment — added v1.2, 2026-07-31)
================================================================================
Before PART 17 (image fork) or PART 20 (text fork) runs, you MUST
classify the subject and write the routing decision to
userData.sculptRuntime.subject.

Shape:
  userData.sculptRuntime.subject = {
    kind: 'object' | 'character' | 'hybrid' | 'creature',
    // For creatures only:
    creaturePlan: 'quadruped' | 'avian' | 'winged-dragon' |
                  'serpentine' | null,
    // For characters and hybrid:
    landmarks: ['face.leftEyeCenter', 'face.rightEyeCenter', ...],
    // For objects and hard-surface hybrid:
    hardSurfaceFaces: ['front', 'side.left', 'side.right',
                       'back', 'top'],
  }

Routing rules:
  - 'object'    → skip landmark rules; PART 20 inventory uses
                  hard-surface faces as regions.
  - 'character' → landmarks REQUIRED; inventory uses body regions
                  (face, torso, limbs, etc.).
  - 'hybrid'    → both faces and body regions in inventory.
  - 'creature'  → landmarks REQUIRED; creaturePlan determines which
                  anatomy proportions apply (quadruped, avian,
                  winged-dragon, serpentine).

================================================================================
PART 23 — POST-RENDER FIDELITY SELF-SCORE  (img2threejs alignment — added v1.2, 2026-07-31)
================================================================================
A factory MUST populate a self-score alongside the model. This is your
own assessment of how well the build matches the reference (or, for
the text-only fork, the prompt). The renderer's Fidelity tab surfaces
it. PART 12 / PART 21 remain pre-output checklists (do this before
emitting); this PART is the post-output score.

Shape:
  userData.sculptRuntime.fidelity = {
    overall: 0.0,             // weighted average (recomputed by
                               // renderer, see below)
    perPass: {                // mirror of passesReviewed from 19.9
      blockout: 0.0, structural: 0.0, form: 0.0, material: 0.0,
      surface: 0.0, lighting: 0.0, interaction: 0.0,
      optimization: 0.0,
    },
    perItem: {                // mirror of detailInventory threshold
      'face.leftEye': 0.0, 'face.rightEye': 0.0, ...
    },
    landmarkIou: 0.0,         // computed by renderer, not you
    styleCoherent: true,      // matches meta.style
    notes: 'short string, max 240 chars',
  }

You populate overall, perPass, perItem, styleCoherent, and notes. The
renderer computes landmarkIou from PART 21 and the rendered silhouette.
overall is recomputed by the renderer as:
    0.45 * mean(perPass) +
    0.30 * mean(perItem) +
    0.25 * landmarkIou
If your reported overall differs from the recomputed overall by more
than 0.15, the renderer flags a "self-score inconsistent" warning.

================================================================================
PART 24 — RENDERER GUARANTEES THAT DO NOT APPLY ON THIS PATH (audit note —
           source: lbl_patch_1_quickwins.txt, cross-checked against
           low_poly_3d.html, 2026-07-29)
================================================================================
A patch describing 4 renderer quick-wins was audited against the actual
source. All 4 turned out to already be shipped — but as part of the
JSON/JS blueprint pipeline (buildScene()/buildMaterialRaw()), which this
TS path deliberately bypasses entirely (PART 1, PART 18.10). Two of the
four do NOT reach a TS-authored model, and that gap is worth stating
explicitly since it's easy to assume a blueprint-side guarantee carries
over here:

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
19.1 — NO AUTOMATIC PER-STYLE TONE MAPPING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The JSON/JS path picks a tone-mapping operator (NoToneMapping, ACES,
Linear, or Cineon) from blueprint.meta.style every time buildScene() runs.
Loading a .ts factory never calls that function — renderer.toneMapping is
left at whatever it was last set to (ACESFilmicToneMapping at
exposure 1.1 on a fresh page load, or a leftover style-driven value if a
JSON/JS blueprint was rendered earlier in the same session). Design PBR
materials (PART 7) assuming ACESFilmic roll-off applies — that's the safe
default a cold-loaded page will actually have — and don't rely on a flat/
unrolled look unless you know the session's tone mapping is already set
that way. There is no TS-side field that requests a specific operator.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
19.2 — NO AUTOMATIC EMISSIVE-INTENSITY CLAMP
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The JSON/JS path soft-warns above 2.5 and hard-clamps emissiveIntensity
at 5.0 before it reaches the material. That clamp lives in
buildMaterialRaw(), which this path never calls — a TS factory sets
`material.emissiveIntensity` directly, and whatever value you write is
what renders, uncapped, with no console warning either way. Self-impose
the same discipline from PART 7.5's material map: 1.0-1.5 soft glow,
2.0-3.0 strong neon, and treat ~5.0 as a practical ceiling even though
nothing enforces it here — under ACESFilmic roll-off (19.1), values much
past that clip to solid white exactly as they would on the JSON/JS path,
just without a warning telling you it happened.

================================================================================
PART 25 — PATCH 3 AUDIT NOTE: PRIMITIVE SUGGESTION APPLIES, THUMBNAILS AND
           EMISSIVE MASK DO NOT (audit note — source:
           lbl_patch_3_bigfeatures.txt, cross-checked against
           low_poly_3d.html, 2026-07-30)
================================================================================
A patch describing 3 renderer big-features (primitive-count suggestion,
reference-library thumbnails, emissive masks) was audited against the
actual source, same as PART 24's audit of the prior patch. Unlike PART
19's items, these three split unevenly across the JSON/JS vs. TS paths —
one applies here untouched, two do not reach a TS-authored model at all.
Same caveat as PART 24: this is easy to get wrong by assuming a blueprint-
side guarantee carries over, so it's stated explicitly below rather than
left implicit.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
20.1 — PRIMITIVE-COUNT SUGGESTION: APPLIES HERE UNCHANGED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The Prompt Builder's reference-image primitive-count estimate (color-
region counting, 32×32 downsample, clamped 3-40) and its TripoSR link are
pure UI behavior that runs the moment a reference image is attached in
the panel — it has no dependency on which output format (JSON, JS, or TS)
the author is about to write, since the analysis happens before any
blueprint or factory code exists. Treat any suggested count exactly as
the JSON/JS-path documentation frames it (Image_To_Json.txt / Prompt_To_
Json.txt PART 60.1): a starting-point hint about how many primitives/
parts/factory-composed sub-shapes the model might need, never a hard
target, and never AI shape reconstruction. Nothing TS-specific to author
here.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
20.2 — REFERENCE LIBRARY THUMBNAILS: DO NOT APPLY TO TS BUILDS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
saveBlueprintToReferenceLibrary() — the function the thumbnail capture
was added to — is called with CURRENT_BLUEPRINT after a build, and no-ops
immediately if that argument is missing meta.style/meta.category. Loading
a .ts factory never populates CURRENT_BLUEPRINT at all (it stays null;
the live model lives in CURRENT_TS_MODEL/CURRENT_TS_META instead, per
PART 1/18.10) — so a TS-authored build is never written to the reference
library, with or without a thumbnail, exactly as it was never saved to
that library before this patch either. This patch changes what gets
stored for JSON/JS saves; it changes nothing about TS builds' total
absence from that library.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
20.3 — EMISSIVE MASK ("emissiveMask"): DO NOT APPLY TO TS BUILDS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Same shape as PART 24.2's emissiveIntensity clamp: the new "emissiveMask"
blueprint field is read and applied inside buildMaterial(), in the same
JSON/JS-only code path this spec's PART 1/18.10 already establishes a TS
factory bypasses entirely. There is no TS-side equivalent field, and
setting `material.emissive`/`material.emissiveIntensity` directly inside
a createXxxModel() factory affects the WHOLE material exactly as it
always has — there is no way to reserve a sub-region (e.g. just the eyes)
for emissive-only glow from a .ts factory. If a model needs a masked
emissive region (the paper's "purple emissive eyes reserved on a separate
map" case), either accept whole-material emissive on this path, or
author the same model as JSON/JS instead where "emissiveMask" is
available (see that pipeline's PART 60.3), or split the masked part out
as its own separate mesh/material within the factory with its own
tighter geometry so its emissive naturally stays confined to the intended
region without needing a texture mask at all.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 26 — v1.1 ADDITIONS: MODELING & TEXTURING TECHNIQUE VOCABULARY AUDIT
          (source: 3d_modeling_texturing_techniques.txt — same 35-technique
          reference list audited in full at Image_To_Json.txt / Prompt_To_
          Json.txt PART 62, cross-checked against this TS path's own
          contract on 2026-07-30)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 62 (see the JSON/JS-path documents) tags each of 35 modeling/
texturing techniques ALREADY LIVE / ALREADY LIVE, partial / ASPIRATIONAL
against the renderer's blueprint-JSON fields and built-in generators. That
tagging does NOT carry over here unchanged, because this path is a
fundamentally different contract: a .ts factory is hand-authored code with
direct access to THREE.* and raw TypeScript/JS, not a JSON field set gated
by what the renderer's parser recognizes. Per PART 1.1/1.2, a factory has
no import path to the renderer's own internal helpers (genFractal,
genLSystem, the CSG builders, etc. are not reachable from a factory file)
— so nothing from PART 62 is "available as a field" here the way it is on
the JSON/JS path. Instead:

  - Every technique PART 62 tags [ALREADY LIVE] or [ALREADY LIVE, partial]
    on the JSON/JS path (parametric modeling, CSG, SDF/metaballs via
    marching cubes, L-systems, fractal displacement, Bezier curves,
    subdivision, noise/fBm/Worley, bump/displacement mapping, projection-
    based UVs, environment mapping, AO via SSAO/cavity vertex-colors) is
    achievable here too, but only by writing the actual Three.js/vanilla-
    JS algorithm inline inside the factory yourself (e.g. real
    THREE.BufferGeometry boolean ops via a bundled CSG library import is
    NOT possible per PART 1.1's no-cross-file-import rule — hand-rolled
    boolean logic on vertex data would be) — there is no shortcut field
    that produces it for you the way "type":"sdf_blob" or
    "texturePattern":"wood" does on the JSON/JS path.
  - Every technique PART 62 tags [ASPIRATIONAL] for the renderer as a
    whole (cellular automata, DLA, reaction-diffusion, Mandelbulb/Menger/
    IFS specifically, wave function collapse, point-cloud reconstruction,
    real physics simulation, neural/AI generation, example-based texture
    synthesis, node-based material graphs) is equally ASPIRATIONAL here —
    a factory's freedom to write arbitrary code doesn't create data
    (scan input, a trained model, a solver) that plainly doesn't exist in
    this project, so these remain out of scope on this path for the same
    reasons PART 62 gives, not new ones.
  - The one path-specific gain: because a factory is real code (not a
    constrained field set), techniques PART 62 marks partial ONLY because
    the JSON side lacks a specific named field (e.g. 62.24's missing
    marble-vein texturePattern, 62.23's missing turbulence function) have
    no equivalent gap here — a factory can implement any procedural
    texture/displacement formula directly as TypeScript, including marble
    veining or turbulence, without waiting on a new renderer field. This
    is the one place this audit's status genuinely differs by path: call
    it [AUTHOR-IMPLEMENTABLE] rather than [ASPIRATIONAL] for anything
    whose only blocker on the JSON/JS side was "no field exposes this
    yet" — the TS path was never blocked by that in the first place.

No new field, helper, or renderer behavior is introduced by this PART; it
is a documentation-only cross-reference, same as PART 62 on the sibling
path.

================================================================================
PART 31 — v1.8 ADDITIONS: CHARACTER / MASCOT SCULPT ENRICHMENT
================================================================================
Source: Character_Sculpt_Enrichment_v1.txt (compiled from object-sculpt
schema 2.1, character-v1.5 track). Compatible with the TS factory path
on top of LBL v1.7 (Prompt_To_Ts.txt / Image_To_Ts.txt).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PURPOSE & SCOPE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This PART is an ADD-ON to the TS output contract in PART 1. It applies
ONLY when the subject is a stylized character, mascot, creature,
humanoid, or anthropomorphic figure. It does NOT replace any of the
core rules in PARTs 1-30 — it adds hierarchy depth, attachment
contracts, action-ready pivots, material frequency bands, recessed-eye
rules, build-pass discipline, and anti-shallow quality gates so the AI
stops emitting flat under-detailed characters in TypeScript.

The TS path already has PART 30 (Rigging & Animation Authoring Standard)
which is the closest existing parallel — this PART 31 is the
character-must-have hierarchy + attachment contract layer that
PART 30's action-ready rig rides on top of.

APPLY automatically when:
  - Subject is character / mascot / creature / humanoid / anthropomorphic
  - Reference image shows a posed or standing figure with limbs, head, face
  - User says "stylized", "mascot", "electric mouse", "chibi", "cartoon
    character", "anthropomorphic", "person", "man", "woman", "kid", etc.
  - Pipeline routing or meta.category is "character"

DO NOT force this structure on pure props, vehicles, architecture, food,
or abstract shapes. A pet rock with eyes is NOT a character; an animal
with bilateral limbs, head, and facial features IS.

For the image-input fork (Image_To_Ts.txt) this PART applies IN
ADDITION to the image analysis protocol in PART 17. The image analysis
protocol determines the proportions and pose; this PART determines the
component hierarchy and attachment contract.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.1 — MANDATORY COMPONENT HIERARCHY (MACRO → MESO → MICRO)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every character factory MUST build at least these named Group nodes
under root (use these exact .name strings or clearly scoped
equivalents). Missing any macro/meso part is a quality failure.

MACRO (required — these are the 6 root-level body masses):
  root            — the returned THREE.Group (set root.name = '')
  pelvis          — child Group .name = 'pelvis'
  abdomen         — child Group .name = 'abdomen'
  chest           — child Group .name = 'chest'
  neck            — child Group .name = 'neck'
  head            — child Group .name = 'head'

MESO (required for full-body characters):
  hair            — child Group .name = 'hair' (may be omitted only if bald)
  clavicle-l / clavicle-r
  upper-arm-l / upper-arm-r
  forearm-l / forearm-r
  hand-l / hand-r
  thigh-l / thigh-r
  shin-l / shin-r
  foot-l / foot-r

MICRO (required for face readability):
  brow-l / brow-r
  ear-l / ear-r
  nose
  mouth
  eye-l / eye-r
  eye-cavity-l / eye-cavity-r   (recessed sockets — see 31.5, never flat decals)

Optional but recommended when visible:
  fingers (thumb/index/middle/ring/little × 2-3 phalanges per hand)
  tail, ears (animal character), horns, accessories, clothing layers,
  held props

RULE: Never collapse independently movable parts into one mesh. Each
limb segment is its own Group with its own named pivot, and the chain
generator from PART 3 / PART 30 is the preferred single-mesh
implementation when the limb needs per-segment continuity — the limb
STILL rides on its own named Group ("arm_l", not folded into "chest").

SKELETON PATTERN (real TypeScript — use this as the factory's spine):

  export function createCharacterModel(opts?: CharacterModelOptions): THREE.Group {
    const root = new THREE.Group();
    root.name = ''; // root intentionally unnamed; see PART 1.2

    // MACRO
    const pelvis  = pivot('pelvis',  [ 0.00, 0.95, 0.00]);
    const abdomen = pivot('abdomen', [ 0.00, 1.20, 0.00]);
    const chest   = pivot('chest',   [ 0.00, 1.45, 0.00]);
    const neck    = pivot('neck',    [ 0.00, 1.70, 0.00]);
    const head    = pivot('head',    [ 0.00, 1.85, 0.00]);

    root.add(pelvis);
    pelvis.add(abdomen);
    abdomen.add(chest);
    chest.add(neck);
    neck.add(head);

    // MESO (left arm — right arm mirrors the same pattern)
    const clavicleL = pivot('clavicle-l', [-0.22, 1.55, 0.00]);
    const armL      = buildArm('l');
    const handL     = buildHand('l');
    chest.add(clavicleL);
    clavicleL.add(armL);
    armL.add(handL); // chain generator already builds pivot geometry

    // MESO (legs)
    const thighL = pivot('thigh-l', [-0.12, 0.85, 0.00]);
    const shinL  = pivot('shin-l',  [-0.12, 0.50, 0.00]);
    const footL  = pivot('foot-l',  [-0.12, 0.05, 0.08]);
    pelvis.add(thighL); thighL.add(shinL); shinL.add(footL);
    // mirror for right side ...

    // MICRO face
    const eyeCavityL = buildEyeCavity('l');
    const eyeL       = buildEye('l');
    head.add(eyeCavityL); eyeCavityL.add(eyeL);
    // ear-l, ear-r, nose, mouth, brow-l, brow-r, hair ...

    // Expose named nodes so the host rig can drive them
    root.userData.sculptRuntime = {
      nodes: {
        root, pelvis, abdomen, chest, neck, head,
        arm_l: armL, hand_l: handL, /* ... */
      },
      sockets: {
        'chest-clavicle-l': clavicleL,
        'chest-clavicle-r': clavicleR,
        'pelvis-thigh-l':   thighL,
        'pelvis-thigh-r':   thighR,
        'head-eye-l':       eyeCavityL,
        'head-eye-r':       eyeCavityR
      },
      materials: { /* see 31.4 */ }
    };

    applyOptions(root, opts);
    return root;
  }

  // Local helper — a named pivot Group at a world position:
  function pivot(name: string, pos: [number, number, number]): THREE.Group {
    const g = new THREE.Group();
    g.name = name;
    g.position.set(pos[0], pos[1], pos[2]);
    return g;
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.2 — ATTACHMENT CONTRACT (userData.attachment on every child mesh)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every child Mesh (or Group that contains a mesh) MUST carry a
`userData.attachment` object describing the seam to its parent. The
TS path's runtime already populates `userData` (PART 6) so this rides
along to the inspector / future rig layer without any renderer change.

PREFERRED SHAPE (TypeScript):

  interface Attachment {
    parentSocket: string;         // semantic socket name on parent
    localStart:   [number, number, number]; // attach point in parent local space
    localEnd:     [number, number, number]; // far end of this segment
    contactType:  'rigid-weld' | 'socket-joint' | 'hinge-joint';
    baseRadius:   number;         // thickness at root
    endRadius:    number;         // thickness at tip
    embedDepth:   number;         // 0.02-0.05
    gapTolerance: number;         // 0.01
  }

CONTACT TYPE GUIDANCE (semantic, not enforced by the renderer):
  rigid-weld    — torso segments, neck, hair, clothing panels
  socket-joint  — shoulder, hip
  hinge-joint   — elbow, knee, finger joints, jaw

Minimum overlap at seams: 0.02 world units (do not leave floating
parts). The existing parent-child Group structure (31.1) is what the
scene graph USES to position the child — the userData.attachment object
is the contract that the rest of the model needs to satisfy.

EXAMPLE — arm_l with a full attachment contract (real TypeScript):

  function buildArm(side: 'l' | 'r'): THREE.Group {
    const sign = side === 'l' ? 1 : -1;
    const arm = new THREE.Group();
    arm.name = `arm_${side}`;
    // chain generator (PART 3) — or compose two capsule segments per
    // PART 40. Pivot is implicit in the segment aim direction.
    const mesh = chainMesh({
      baseRadius: 0.07,
      segs: 6,
      segments: [
        { length: 0.32, tipRatio: 0.85, aim: [ 0.06 * sign, -1, 0.05] },
        { length: 0.30, tipRatio: 0.65, aim: [ 0.08 * sign, -1, 0.20] }
      ]
    }, M.skin);
    arm.add(mesh);
    arm.userData.attachment = {
      parentSocket: 'chest-clavicle-' + side,
      localStart:   [ 0.22 * sign, -0.05,  0.00],
      localEnd:     [ 0.26 * sign, -0.65,  0.05],
      contactType:  'socket-joint',
      baseRadius:   0.085,
      endRadius:    0.055,
      embedDepth:   0.04,
      gapTolerance: 0.01
    };
    return arm;
  }

  // Quick helper for a "weld" attachment (clothing panels, hair):
  function weldAttachment(
    localStart: [number, number, number],
    localEnd:   [number, number, number],
    baseR: number, endR: number
  ): Attachment {
    return {
      parentSocket: 'auto',
      localStart, localEnd,
      contactType: 'rigid-weld',
      baseRadius: baseR, endRadius: endR,
      embedDepth: 0.03, gapTolerance: 0.01
    };
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.3 — ACTION PROFILE & PIVOTS (userData.actionProfile on every macro/meso)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every macro and meso Group/Mesh should carry a
`userData.actionProfile` object. This is the same shape PART 30's rig
code reads — keep it in sync so a host rig can drive the character
without re-deriving it.

PREFERRED SHAPE (TypeScript):

  type AnimationRole = 'root' | 'static' | 'limb' | 'digit' | 'face';
  interface ActionProfile {
    animationRole: AnimationRole;
    pivot: {
      mode: 'center' | 'root' | 'custom';
      localPosition: [number, number, number];
      axis:           [number, number, number];
    };
    transformChannels: {
      translate:     boolean;
      rotate:        boolean;
      scale:         boolean;
      bend:          boolean;
      twist:         boolean;
      detach:        boolean;
      visibility:    boolean;
      materialState: boolean;
    };
    collider: {
      type:   'box' | 'sphere' | 'capsule';
      offset: [number, number, number];
      scale:  [number, number, number];
    };
    destruction: {
      breakable:     boolean;
      fractureGroup: string;
    };
  }

PIVOT PLACEMENT RULES (read these BEFORE setting pivot.localPosition):
  root        — at the model's logical center (usually 0,0,0)
  pelvis      — at the hip center
  abdomen     — at the lower torso center
  chest       — at the upper torso center
  neck        — at the base of the neck (jaw line)
  head        — at the head center (NOT at the chin)
  hair        — at the scalp root
  upper-arm   — at the shoulder (pivot at the join, not the segment middle)
  forearm     — at the elbow
  hand        — at the wrist
  thigh       — at the hip
  shin        — at the knee
  foot        — at the ankle
  eye         — at the eye center
  jaw/mouth   — at the lower-lip center

EXAMPLE — head with actionProfile (real TypeScript):

  function buildHead(): THREE.Group {
    const head = new THREE.Group();
    head.name = 'head';
    head.position.set(0, 1.85, 0);
    const sphere = new THREE.Mesh(
      new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.18, 18, 14),
      M.skin
    );
    sphere.name = 'head-mesh';
    head.add(sphere);
    head.userData.actionProfile = {
      animationRole: 'face',
      pivot: { mode: 'center', localPosition: [0, 0, 0], axis: [0, 1, 0] },
      transformChannels: {
        translate: true, rotate: true, scale: false,
        bend: false, twist: false, detach: false,
        visibility: true, materialState: false
      },
      collider: { type: 'sphere', offset: [0, 0, 0], scale: [1, 1, 1] },
      destruction: { breakable: false, fractureGroup: 'head' }
    };
    return head;
  }

  // Helper for the common "limb joint" case:
  function jointActionProfile(
    role: AnimationRole,
    axis: [number, number, number] = [0, 1, 0]
  ): ActionProfile {
    return {
      animationRole: role,
      pivot: { mode: 'root', localPosition: [0, 0, 0], axis },
      transformChannels: {
        translate: true, rotate: true, scale: false,
        bend: true, twist: false, detach: false,
        visibility: true, materialState: false
      },
      collider: { type: 'capsule', offset: [0, -0.15, 0], scale: [1, 1, 1] },
      destruction: { breakable: false, fractureGroup: role }
    };
  }

For chain-generator limbs the pivot is implicit in the segment aim
direction; you can still fill in actionProfile with the joint pivot
position (the elbow) for the forearm segment specifically.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.4 — MATERIALS (character palette + frequency bands)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Minimum material set for a clothed character:
  skin, hair, shirt (or torso cloth), pants, shoes, eye, lips

A creature/mascot may substitute "fur"/"feathers"/"scales" for skin +
hair, but keep the eye + a "mouth interior" material distinct.

The TS path's MeshStandardMaterial already supports color / roughness /
metalness / emissive / normalMap. The "surface frequency bands" from
the enrichment file are extra OPTIONAL fields the runtime ignores today
— they're documented here for future rig/timing layers:

  - albedo.dominant        — MeshStandardMaterial.color (already set)
  - albedo.secondary       — optional "secondary color" for hand-painted
                              trim panels (renderer-ignored; document intent)
  - roughness.base         — MeshStandardMaterial.roughness (already set)
  - roughness.variation    — optional 0.0-0.1 jitter amount
  - surfaceFrequencyBands: — object with three optional sub-fields
      macro  — broad color/height breakup (sampled region span)
      meso   — pores, fabric weave, fur clumps
      micro  — highlight breakup under grazing light

DO NOT use a single flat color for the whole character. DO NOT paint
lighting into albedo. If you want a lit-vs-shadow region, use one of:
  - A second MeshStandardMaterial with slightly darker color on the
    specific panel/sub-mesh that's genuinely recessed
  - A duplicated, slightly inset mesh under a "shadow" Group
  - A custom onBeforeCompile hook (PART 28) for vertex-driven shading

ROUGHNESS GUIDANCE (when you set it explicitly on
MeshStandardMaterial.roughness):
  skin   0.45 - 0.60
  cloth  0.70 - 0.90
  hair   0.55 - 0.75
  eye    0.15 - 0.25
  lips   0.30 - 0.50
  shoe   0.50 - 0.80

EXAMPLE — character materials in the M object (drop into buildMaterials()
from PART 7):

  const M = {
    skin: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xe8b87c, roughness: 0.55, metalness: 0.0 }),
    skin_d: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xb5773a, roughness: 0.65, metalness: 0.0 }),
    hair: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x3a2418, roughness: 0.65, metalness: 0.0 }),
    shirt: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xcc2222, roughness: 0.80, metalness: 0.0 }),
    pants: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x1a3a8a, roughness: 0.85, metalness: 0.0 }),
    shoe: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x1a1a1a, roughness: 0.70, metalness: 0.0 }),
    eye: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x1a1a1a, roughness: 0.20, metalness: 0.05 }),
    lip: new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xcc6644, roughness: 0.40, metalness: 0.0 })
  };

  // For meshes that need per-vertex tint jitter, add colorVariation
  // as a custom onBeforeCompile hook (PART 28.1) or a baked CanvasTexture
  // (PART 8) — don't try to add an LBL "colorVariation" field on a
  // MeshStandardMaterial directly.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.5 — FACE LANDMARKS & RECESSED EYES (critical)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Eyes MUST read as recessed cavities, not flat stickers. PREFERRED
techniques, in order of preference:

  1. Smaller sphere for the cavity, then a slightly smaller eye sphere
     inset into it (most reliable across every renderer version).
  2. Inset sphere with a slight negative Z offset (chibi style).
  3. booleanSubtract a small sphere from the head, then place a smaller
     eye sphere inside the resulting hole. (Use the three-bvh-csg
     library for true boolean ops, or use ExtrudeGeometry with a
     pierced Shape from PART 3.7.)

LANDMARK PLACEMENT (relative to head height, where head height = 1.0):
  eye line          — at   0.55 of head height from the chin
  eye spacing       — 0.30-0.35 of head width apart (center to center)
  nose base         — at   0.40 of head height from the chin
  mouth line        — at   0.25 of head height from the chin
  brow line         — at   0.68 of head height (above the eye line)
  ear line          — at   0.55 of head height (≈ on the eye line)

EXAMPLE — recessed eye cluster (chibi, head height 0.42, real TS):

  function buildEyeCavity(side: 'l' | 'r'): THREE.Group {
    const sign = side === 'l' ? -1 : 1;
    const g = new THREE.Group();
    g.name = `eye-cavity-${side}`;
    g.position.set(0.06 * sign, 0.04, 0.18);
    const m = new THREE.Mesh(
      new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.045, 12, 10),
      M.skin_d
    );
    m.name = `eye-cavity-${side}-mesh`;
    g.add(m);
    g.userData.actionProfile = {
      animationRole: 'face',
      pivot: { mode: 'center', localPosition: [0, 0, 0], axis: [0, 1, 0] },
      transformChannels: {
        translate: false, rotate: false, scale: false,
        bend: false, twist: false, detach: false,
        visibility: true, materialState: false
      },
      collider: { type: 'sphere', offset: [0, 0, 0], scale: [1, 1, 1] },
      destruction: { breakable: false, fractureGroup: 'head' }
    };
    return g;
  }

  function buildEye(side: 'l' | 'r'): THREE.Group {
    const g = new THREE.Group();
    g.name = `eye-${side}`;
    g.position.set(0, 0, 0.005);
    const m = new THREE.Mesh(
      new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.034, 12, 10),
      M.eye
    );
    m.name = `eye-${side}-mesh`;
    g.add(m);
    return g;
  }

  // In the head builder:
  head.add(buildEyeCavity('l'));
  head.children.find(c => c.name === 'eye-cavity-l')!.add(buildEye('l'));
  // ... mirror for right side ...

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.6 — BUILD PASSES (ordered, do not skip)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
When generating a character factory, mentally (or actually) follow this
order. Emit the full factory only after the last required pass.

  1. blockout
     - root + pelvis + abdomen + chest + neck + head + major limb capsules
     - Correct head-unit proportions and overall silhouette
     - Acceptance: humanoid proportions readable with zero materials

  2. proportion-lock
     - Lock head/torso/limb ratios to measured reference (or style table)
     - Fix pose angles (elbow/knee bend, weight shift)

  3. feature-placement
     - hair, brows, ears, nose, mouth, eyes + cavities
     - fingers if visible
     - All attachment contracts filled (31.2)

  4. material-pass
     - Assign skin/hair/cloth/shoe/eye/lips materials
     - Sample real mid-tone colors from reference if image is present
     - Add roughness variation and basic frequency bands (31.4)

  5. lighting-pass
     - Optional in the factory, required in the post-render review
     - Note key / fill / rim intent (which way does the main light come
       from on the reference?)

  6. interaction-pass
     - Confirm every limb has a named pivot in userData.actionProfile (31.3)
     - Confirm every held prop is parented to a hand Group, not floating
     - Populate root.userData.sculptRuntime with nodes + sockets (31.1)

  7. optimization-pass
     - Stay inside the performance budget (prefer < 80-120k tris for
       full characters)
     - Share material instances across sibling meshes (one M.skin,
       not 14 copies)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.7 — QUALITY CONTRACT (anti-shallow rules — REJECT/REWRITE if any fire)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
REJECT the factory and rewrite if ANY of these are true:

  - Only a root + 1-2 child Groups for a full character (PART 31.1 lists
    the minimum set; a 3-Group character is NEVER acceptable)
  - Limbs are single CapsuleGeometry meshes with no elbow/knee break
    (use the chain generator from PART 3, or two stacked capsule
    segments per limb)
  - Eyes are flat PlaneGeometry / decal-style quads with no cavity (31.5)
  - Hands are undifferentiated BoxGeometry with no fingers (when
    fingers are visible at the reference's zoom level)
  - Materials are one generic MeshStandardMaterial with uniform
    roughness (31.4)
  - Child Groups float with no parent chain back to root (31.1)
  - Adjacent parts have seam gap > 0.02 (PART 30 rule)
  - Face landmarks ignore measured eye-line / mouth-line (31.5)
  - Pose is forced to perfect T-pose when the reference shows a
    different pose (PART 30 / PART 42)
  - root.userData.sculptRuntime.nodes does not name every macro +
    meso Group by its standard id (31.1 / 31.8)

DEFINITION OF DONE (character):
  Silhouette matches the reference, primary proportions are correct,
  component hierarchy depth ≥ 3 levels (macro + meso + micro from 31.1),
  most recognizable local features are present, materials respond to
  light (not flat plastic), root.userData.sculptRuntime exposes
  named nodes and sockets.

Append these to PART 12's validation checklist (character-only items):

  [ ] [v1.8] Character has all 6 MACRO Groups (root, pelvis, abdomen,
      chest, neck, head) under root — 31.1.
  [ ] [v1.8] Full-body character has all 14 MESO Groups (hair, 2× clavicle,
      2× upper-arm, 2× forearm, 2× hand, 2× thigh, 2× shin, 2× foot) —
      omit only the parts genuinely not visible — 31.1.
  [ ] [v1.8] Face has eye-cavity-l, eye-l, eye-cavity-r, eye-r as 4
      separate Groups (NOT one merged "eyes" object) — 31.5.
  [ ] [v1.8] Every child limb/appendage has either a userData.attachment
      object (31.2) OR a parent Group with the right world-space
      overlap to its child.
  [ ] [v1.8] Every macro and meso Group has a userData.actionProfile
      with at least animationRole + pivot (31.3) — even if the host
      rig doesn't drive it today.
  [ ] [v1.8] Minimum material set is present (skin, hair, torso cloth,
      pants/legs, shoes, eye) — 31.4. A mascot/creature may substitute
      fur/feathers/scales for skin + hair but still needs eye +
      mouth-interior distinct.
  [ ] [v1.8] The eye MeshStandardMaterial has roughness < 0.3 and
      metalness < 0.1 — not a generic PBR preset — 31.4 / 31.5.
  [ ] [v1.8] root.userData.sculptRuntime.nodes names every macro +
      meso Group by its standard id (pelvis, chest, arm_l, etc.) — 31.1.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.8 — OUTPUT RULES (still pure TS)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - File is a real ES module (PART 1.1): `import * as THREE from 'three'`
    at the top, an `export function createXxxModel(...)` factory, an
    optional `export function createXxxModelLookDevLights(...)`.
  - The factory returns a THREE.Group with root.name = '' and the
    6 MACRO Groups added under it (31.1).
  - root.userData.sculptRuntime (PART 6) is REQUIRED and must include
    at minimum: nodes (every macro + meso Group by standard id) and
    sockets (the named attach points a host rig / animation system
    can target).
  - userData.attachment + userData.actionProfile are EXTRA fields on
    individual Group/Mesh nodes. The current renderer ignores unknown
    userData keys safely; future rig/animation layers consume them.
  - When an image is supplied, MEASURE proportions and SAMPLE colors
    from the image before writing any numbers (see Image_To_Ts.txt
    PART 17 for the full protocol).
  - Every limb segment is its own Group with its own id and pivot —
    the chain generator (PART 3) is the preferred single-mesh
    implementation but the limb Group still exists.
  - Keep all existing TS path constraints: no top-level DOM access
    outside the factory, no network fetches, no `eval`, no CommonJS
    `module.exports` (PART 1.5).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31.9 — QUICK CHARACTER SCAFFOLD (copy-paste starting point)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Use this mental skeleton, then fill real scales/positions from the
reference (or from PART 20's style table for a prompt-only build):

  root
  ├── pelvis
  │   ├── thigh-l → shin-l → foot-l
  │   └── thigh-r → shin-r → foot-r
  ├── abdomen
  │   └── chest
  │       ├── neck → head
  │       │         ├── hair
  │       │         ├── brow-l / brow-r
  │       │         ├── ear-l / ear-r
  │       │         ├── nose
  │       │         ├── mouth
  │       │         ├── eye-cavity-l → eye-l
  │       │         └── eye-cavity-r → eye-r
  │       ├── clavicle-l → upper-arm-l → forearm-l → hand-l (+ fingers)
  │       └── clavicle-r → upper-arm-r → forearm-r → hand-r (+ fingers)

For a chibi mascot, fold thighs and shins into one stubby cylinder per
leg, omit fingers, exaggerate the head, and keep all 6 MACRO Groups
(31.1 — never fold chest+abdomen+pelvis into one "body" even on a
chibi).

For a quadruped (dog, cat, horse, dragon), replace thigh-r/l + shin-r/l
with front-leg-r/l + back-leg-r/l, add a tail Group attached to
pelvis, and keep the same eye/brow/ear/nose/mouth micro face structure
(move ears forward to the eye line per 31.5).

For a creature with no arms (slime, octopus with a humanoid face), drop
clavicle/upper-arm/forearm/hand per side but keep the rest of the
hierarchy intact.

================================================================================
END OF PART 31 — CHARACTER / MASCOT SCULPT ENRICHMENT
Output remains valid TS factory module. Unknown extra fields (userData.
attachment, userData.actionProfile, userData.surfaceFrequencyBands,
etc.) are kept as optional and ignored by the current renderer; they
exist so future rig/animation layers can consume them, and so the AI
is forced to author a proper component hierarchy instead of a flat
1-2-Group character. The rules in this PART apply ONLY when the
subject is a character, mascot, creature, or humanoid.
================================================================================

================================================================================
PART 32 — v1.9 ADDITIONS: THREE.JS STRUCTURAL ERROR CORRECTIONS
(Diagnostic & Anti-Pattern Hard Rules — System Diagnostic Report)
================================================================================
Source: SYSTEM_DIAGNOSTIC_AND_ARCHITECTURAL_ERROR_REPORT_THREEJS.txt
(captured errors 1-10 from prompt-to-ts, img2threejs, WebGL sandboxes, and
glTF 2.0 exporter pipelines). This PART formalizes the 10 fixes as hard
rules so the AI never re-emits any of the structural failures below.
The rules reinforce — never contradict — PARTs 1, 4, 5, 7, 8, 15, and 30.
Where PART 30 already covers a concept in depth, this PART is the
single-line "do not violate" reminder with the canonical anti-pattern vs
compliant-pattern example; cross-reference the parent PART for full
treatment.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32.1 — ANTI-PATTERN CATALOG (all 10 — verbatim, do not rephrase away)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[1] RELATIVE LOCAL IMPORT SPECIFIERS IN STANDALONE MODULES
    ❌ import { createLegGeometry } from './geometry';
    ❌ import { buildWalkAnimation } from './animations';
    ❌ import { DEFAULT_COLORS }     from './constants';
    ✅ import * as THREE from 'three';
    ✅ import { GLTFExporter } from 'three/examples/jsm/exporters/GLTFExporter.js';
    RULE: every .ts factory is a SINGLE self-contained file. The only
    allowed imports are 'three' and 'three/examples/jsm/...' (or the
    'three/addons/...' alias the renderer's import map accepts — see
    PART 4.1). ZERO relative imports — not `./`, not `../`, not anything
    that would fail in a blob-URL/worker runner. Everything (constants,
    geometries, materials, helpers, animation tracks, GLB export helper)
    lives IN-FILE.

[2] ANIMATION TRACK TARGET SPECIFIERS & HIERARCHY BINDING FAILURES
    ❌ const leftThigh = new THREE.Bone(); // .name never set!
    ❌ new THREE.QuaternionKeyframeTrack(
           'armature/left_thigh/rotation',   // wrong path prefix
           times, quaternionValues);
    ✅ const leftThigh = new THREE.Bone();
       leftThigh.name = 'Bone_Thigh_L';     // explicit, alphanumeric
       rootGroup.add(leftThigh);
       new THREE.QuaternionKeyframeTrack(
           'Bone_Thigh_L.quaternion',        // exact "NodeName.prop"
           times, quaternionValues);
    RULE: every animated node has an explicit alphanumeric .name. Every
    KeyframeTrack name is exactly "<NodeName>.<property>" — no path
    prefix, no spaces, no special characters. All animated bones are
    attached to the root group BEFORE the AnimationMixer(root) is
    constructed (PART 30.2-30.5).

[3] UN-NORMALIZED QUATERNIONS & SLERP INTERPOLATION JITTER
    ❌ const q = new THREE.Quaternion().setFromEuler(
                     new THREE.Euler(0.5, 0.2, 0));
       values.push(q.x, q.y, q.z, q.w);    // floating-point drift
    ✅ const tempEuler = new THREE.Euler();
       const tempQuat  = new THREE.Quaternion();
       function pushQuatKeyframe(x, y, z, arr) {
         tempEuler.set(x, y, z, 'XYZ');
         tempQuat.setFromEuler(tempEuler).normalize();
         arr.push(tempQuat.x, tempQuat.y, tempQuat.z, tempQuat.w);
       }
    RULE: always call .normalize() after setFromEuler before pushing
    quaternion values. Times array MUST be strictly monotonic
    (0 <= t0 < t1 < ... < tn). For looping clips, frame 0 must equal
    frame N (same quaternion components). The pushQuatKeyframe helper
    above is the canonical pattern — keep one per file, reuse it.

[4] BONE PIVOT MISALIGNMENT & DISLOCATION (LOCAL VS. WORLD SPACE)
    ❌ // world-space positions, both added to root — calf detaches on rotate
       const thigh = new THREE.Bone();
       thigh.position.set(0.6, 1.2, 0);
       const calf = new THREE.Bone();
       calf.position.set(0.6, 0.6, 0);
       rootGroup.add(thigh);
       rootGroup.add(calf);
    ✅ // proper local-offset hierarchy
       const boneThigh = new THREE.Bone();
       boneThigh.name = 'Bone_Thigh_L';
       boneThigh.position.set(0.6, 1.2, 0);     // local to pelvis
       rootPelvis.add(boneThigh);
       const boneCalf = new THREE.Bone();
       boneCalf.name = 'Bone_Calf_L';
       boneCalf.position.set(0, -0.6, 0.15);    // LOCAL to thigh joint
       boneThigh.add(boneCalf);
    RULE: strict parent-child transform hierarchy (Root -> Pelvis ->
    Torso -> HipSocket -> Thigh -> Calf -> Foot). Child .position is
    ALWAYS a local offset relative to the immediate parent. Limb mesh
    geometry has its rotation pivot at (0,0,0) in local mesh space —
    use geometry.translate() to anchor vertices to the joint origin
    before attaching to a bone.

[5] PROCEDURAL CANVAS DECAL CONTEXT LIFECYCLE & MISSING FLAGS
    ❌ const canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
       const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
       ctx.fillText('42', 10, 10);
       const texture = new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);
       // missing: needsUpdate, colorSpace
    ✅ if (typeof document === 'undefined') return null;
       const canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
       canvas.width = 256; canvas.height = 256;
       const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
       if (!ctx) return null;
       ctx.clearRect(0, 0, 256, 256);
       ctx.fillStyle = '#ffffff';
       ctx.font = 'bold 120px sans-serif';
       ctx.textAlign = 'center';
       ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
       ctx.fillText(label, 128, 128);
       const texture = new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);
       texture.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;   // r150+ replacement
       texture.needsUpdate = true;
       return texture;
    RULE: guard `typeof document === 'undefined'` for SSR/Node test
    paths. Guard `if (!ctx) return null;`. Always set both
    `texture.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace` and
    `texture.needsUpdate = true` immediately after CanvasTexture
    construction. Reinforces PART 8.

[6] GLTF 2.0 / GLTFEXPORTER SERIALIZATION INCOMPATIBILITIES
    ❌ mesh.userData = {
         parentReference: rootGroup,    // CIRCULAR ref crashes exporter
         onClick: () => console.log('clicked'),  // function breaks JSON
       };
    ✅ mesh.userData = {
         partName: 'Chassis_Main',      // pure JSON-safe scalar fields
         isMechanicalLimb: true,
       };
       // Animations embedded on the root group:
       rootGroup.animations = clips;
       export async function exportRiggedModelToGlb(
         rootGroup, clips, filename = 'model.glb'
       ): Promise<ArrayBuffer> {
         const exporter = new GLTFExporter();
         return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
           exporter.parse(
             rootGroup,
             gltf => resolve(gltf as ArrayBuffer),
             err   => reject(err),
             { binary: true, animations: clips }
           );
         });
       }
    RULE: userData MUST be JSON-serializable (plain strings, numbers,
    booleans, arrays, plain objects). No functions, no EventListeners,
    no circular parent references. Embed baked clips in
    `rootGroup.animations = clips`. The standalone
    `exportRiggedModelToGlb(root, clips, filename)` helper above is
    the canonical pattern — keep one per file, even if the AI never
    wires it to UI; it documents the export contract and survives in
    the inspector console.

[7] DEPRECATED THREE.JS LEGACY APIs (r150+ COMPATIBILITY)
    ❌ new THREE.Geometry();                     // REMOVED in r125+
    ❌ geometry.computeFaceNormals();            // REMOVED
    ❌ texture.encoding = THREE.sRGBEncoding;    // DEPRECATED in r152
    ✅ new THREE.BoxGeometry(), CylinderGeometry(), ExtrudeGeometry(),
       SphereGeometry(), IcosahedronGeometry(), CapsuleGeometry(),
       RoundedBoxGeometry(), ... (always BufferGeometry subclasses)
    ✅ geometry.computeVertexNormals();
    ✅ texture.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;
    ✅ renderer.outputColorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;
    ✅ MeshStandardMaterial / MeshPhysicalMaterial only (PBR)
    RULE: target Three.js r150+ exclusively. No THREE.Geometry, no
    THREE.Face3, no computeFaceNormals, no sRGBEncoding, no
    LambertMaterial / PhongMaterial / BasicMaterial as the primary
    surface shader. Reinforces PART 7 and PART 28.

[8] MATERIAL/GEOMETRY SHARED MUTATION & MEMORY LEAKAGE
    ❌ // same material on left + right leg, then mutate color on one
       const legMat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xc8763a });
       leftLegMesh.material  = legMat;
       rightLegMesh.material = legMat;
       leftLegMesh.material.color.set(0xff0000);   // also recolors right
    ✅ // distinct material instances for distinct visual roles
       const chassisMat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xc8763a });
       const metalMat   = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
         color: 0xb8b8b8, metalness: 0.85, roughness: 0.35
       });
       // mirror limbs via cloned geometry, not shared material:
       const leftGeom  = baseGeom.clone();
       const rightGeom = baseGeom.clone();
       // always export a dispose() helper that walks the tree:
       export function disposeRig(rootGroup: THREE.Object3D): void {
         rootGroup.traverse(obj => {
           const m = obj as THREE.Mesh;
           if (m.isMesh) {
             m.geometry?.dispose();
             const mat = m.material as
               THREE.Material | THREE.Material[] | undefined;
             if (Array.isArray(mat)) mat.forEach(x => x.dispose());
             else if (mat) mat.dispose();
           }
         });
       }
    RULE: distinct material per visual role. Mirror limbs via cloned
    geometry (leftGeom = baseGeom.clone()), not via shared material
    mutation. Always provide a dispose() helper that traverses the
    tree and disposes geometries + materials + textures (the
    inspector can invoke it on model swap).

[9] TYPESCRIPT TYPE INCOHERENCE & MISSING RETURN CONTRACTS
    ❌ export function createMech(opts) {   // no return type, no opts type
         return new THREE.Group();
       }
    ✅ export interface ModelOptions {
         /** overall scale multiplier (default 1) */
         scale?: number;
         /** enable cast/receive shadows (default true) */
         shadows?: boolean;
         /** show as wireframe (default false) — used by the inspector */
         wireframe?: boolean;
         // model-specific typed fields below
       }
       export interface ModelBuildResult {
         rootGroup: THREE.Group;
         bones: Map<string, THREE.Bone>;
         materials: Record<string, THREE.Material>;
         clips: THREE.AnimationClip[];
         dispose: () => void;
       }
       export interface MeshTriangleMetrics {
         triangles: number;
         meshes:    number;
         materials: number;
         bones:     number;
         clips:     number;
       }
    RULE: every public surface is typed. `export interface XxxOptions`
    on the factory's options arg. `export interface XxxBuildResult`
    when the factory returns more than just the THREE.Group (e.g.,
    rigged models). `export interface XxxMeshTriangleMetrics` for any
    metrics helper. Reinforces PART 2.

[10] INCONSISTENT FACTORY ENTRYPOINT SIGNATURES
    ❌ // output one day calls it createModel, next day buildModel, then init
       export function createModel(opts) { ... }
       // and the file has no create*Model() export → loader rejects
    ✅ // ALWAYS export BOTH the named primary builder AND a default alias
       export function buildMechWalker(
         options?: MechWalkerOptions
       ): ModelBuildResult {
         // … build the model …
         return { rootGroup, bones, materials, clips, dispose };
       }
       // Universal alias — every renderer's loader pattern picks this up
       export function createMechWalkerModel(
         options?: MechWalkerOptions
       ): THREE.Group {
         return buildMechWalker(options).rootGroup;
       }
       export default buildMechWalker;
    RULE: every TS factory file exports a function matching
    /^create\w+Model$/ (the renderer's loader pattern — PART 1.2). For
    non-character models that need a richer return (bones + clips +
    metrics), ALSO export a named primary builder (buildXxx) plus a
    default export. The loader-facing name is the canonical entry
    point; the richer builder is for tests and the inspector.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32.2 — CANONICAL HELPERS (copy these into every TS file as needed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
These four helpers collectively close errors [3], [5], [6], [8] when
included in a file. Drop them in verbatim; do not rewrite.

  // ---- Quaternion keyframe pusher (closes error [3]) -----------------
  const _euler = new THREE.Euler();
  const _quat  = new THREE.Quaternion();
  function pushQuatKeyframe(
    xRad: number, yRad: number, zRad: number, arr: number[]
  ): void {
    _euler.set(xRad, yRad, zRad, 'XYZ');
    _quat.setFromEuler(_euler).normalize();
    arr.push(_quat.x, _quat.y, _quat.z, _quat.w);
  }

  // ---- Canvas decal texture (closes error [5]) -----------------------
  export function createDecalTexture(
    label = '42',
    size  = 256
  ): THREE.CanvasTexture | null {
    if (typeof document === 'undefined') return null;
    const canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
    canvas.width = size; canvas.height = size;
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
    if (!ctx) return null;
    ctx.clearRect(0, 0, size, size);
    ctx.fillStyle    = '#ffffff';
    ctx.font         = `bold ${Math.floor(size * 0.45)}px sans-serif`;
    ctx.textAlign    = 'center';
    ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
    ctx.fillText(label, size / 2, size / 2);
    const tex = new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);
    tex.colorSpace  = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;
    tex.needsUpdate = true;
    return tex;
  }

  // ---- GLB export helper (closes error [6]) --------------------------
  export function exportRiggedModelToGlb(
    rootGroup: THREE.Group,
    clips: THREE.AnimationClip[],
    filename = 'model.glb'
  ): Promise<ArrayBuffer> {
    const exporter = new GLTFExporter();
    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
      exporter.parse(
        rootGroup,
        gltf => resolve(gltf as ArrayBuffer),
        err   => reject(err),
        { binary: true, animations: clips }
      );
    });
  }

  // ---- Full-tree dispose (closes error [8]) --------------------------
  export function disposeRig(rootGroup: THREE.Object3D): void {
    rootGroup.traverse(obj => {
      const m = obj as THREE.Mesh;
      if (!m.isMesh) return;
      m.geometry?.dispose();
      const mat = m.material as
        THREE.Material | THREE.Material[] | undefined;
      if (Array.isArray(mat)) mat.forEach(x => x.dispose());
      else if (mat) mat.dispose();
    });
  }

  // ---- Inspect metrics (closes error [9] + feeds the inspector) ------
  export function inspectModelTriangles(
    rootGroup: THREE.Object3D
  ): MeshTriangleMetrics {
    let triangles = 0, meshes = 0;
    const materials = new Set<THREE.Material>();
    rootGroup.traverse(obj => {
      const m = obj as THREE.Mesh;
      if (!m.isMesh) return;
      meshes++;
      const idx = m.geometry?.getIndex();
      const pos = m.geometry?.getAttribute('position');
      if (idx) triangles += idx.count / 3;
      else if (pos) triangles += pos.count / 3;
      const mat = m.material as
        THREE.Material | THREE.Material[] | undefined;
      if (Array.isArray(mat)) mat.forEach(x => materials.add(x));
      else if (mat) materials.add(mat);
    });
    const bones  = new Set<THREE.Bone>();
    const clips  = new Set<THREE.AnimationClip>();
    rootGroup.traverse(obj => {
      if ((obj as THREE.Bone).isBone) bones.add(obj as THREE.Bone);
    });
    const root = rootGroup as THREE.Group;
    (root.animations || []).forEach(c => clips.add(c));
    return {
      triangles: Math.round(triangles),
      meshes,
      materials: materials.size,
      bones:     bones.size,
      clips:     clips.size,
    };
  }

  export interface MeshTriangleMetrics {
    triangles: number;
    meshes:    number;
    materials: number;
    bones:     number;
    clips:     number;
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32.3 — STRUCTURAL REFERENCE CHECKLIST (run through silently before output)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Before you emit a .ts factory, mentally walk this list. Any unchecked
item is a build failure on the renderer's TS loader.

  [ ] 1. ZERO relative local imports (`./...` or `../...`).
  [ ] 2. All imports strictly from 'three' or 'three/examples/jsm/...'
        (or the 'three/addons/...' alias the import map accepts).
  [ ] 3. Every animated Bone has an explicit, matching .name string.
  [ ] 4. All QuaternionKeyframeTracks are normalized and use
        "BoneName.quaternion" — no path prefix, no spaces.
  [ ] 5. Child bones are parented to their respective parent bones
        with LOCAL offsets (not world coordinates).
  [ ] 6. Every animated node is attached to the root group BEFORE
        `new THREE.AnimationMixer(root)` is constructed.
  [ ] 7. All quaternion keyframe arrays are strictly monotonic in
        time, and the first/last frames match for looping clips.
  [ ] 8. All materials use MeshStandardMaterial (or MeshPhysicalMaterial
        for clearcoat/transmission) with valid PBR values; no
        LambertMaterial/PhongMaterial/BasicMaterial as primary.
  [ ] 9. Every CanvasTexture has both `needsUpdate = true` and
        `colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace`.
  [ ] 10. userData is free of functions, EventListeners, and circular
         parent references — pure JSON-serializable values only.
  [ ] 11. All AnimationClips are embedded in `rootGroup.animations`
         (or passed directly to GLTFExporter.parse).
  [ ] 12. Distinct material instances for distinct visual roles;
         mirrored limbs use cloned geometry, not shared material.
  [ ] 13. A `dispose()` helper exists that walks the tree and disposes
         geometry + materials + textures.
  [ ] 14. Public surfaces are typed: XxxOptions, XxxBuildResult,
         MeshTriangleMetrics — no implicit `any`.
  [ ] 15. The file exports a function matching /^create\w+Model$/
         (the renderer's loader pattern) AND (for rigged models) a
         named `buildXxx()` + `export default`.
  [ ] 16. Three.js r150+ only — no THREE.Geometry, no
         computeFaceNormals, no sRGBEncoding.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32.4 — INTERSECTION WITH EXISTING PARTS (read this once, then trust them)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This PART does NOT replace any existing rule — it consolidates and
references them so a new AI session that hasn't fully absorbed PART 30
still cannot emit broken code. The 10 errors above are addressed in
depth at the following existing locations; cross-reference is in [brackets]:

  [1] single-file architecture          → PART 1.1, PART 4 (entire)
  [2] bone .name + track binding         → PART 30.2-30.5
  [3] normalized quaternions             → PART 30.3
  [4] local bone offsets                 → PART 30.2, PART 31.1-31.3
  [5] canvas texture lifecycle           → PART 8 (entire)
  [6] GLB export / userData hygiene      → PART 1.5, PART 30.5, PART 30.7
  [7] r150+ API compatibility            → PART 3, PART 7, PART 28
  [8] material / geometry instance split → PART 7, PART 15
  [9] TypeScript type contracts          → PART 2, PART 1.2
  [10] factory entrypoint signature      → PART 1.2, PART 30.0, PART 30.6

PART 32 is the safety net for any PART above that an AI session might
have skimmed. If you remember ONLY one thing from this file, remember
the 10-line catalog in 32.1 — the rest is depth.

================================================================================
END OF PART 32 — THREE.JS STRUCTURAL ERROR CORRECTIONS
Output remains a valid TS factory module. The 4 canonical helpers in
32.2 are OPTIONAL inclusions — drop them in if and only if the model
actually exercises the corresponding feature (a procedural decal, a
rig, a GLB export path, a metrics inspector). The structural checklist
in 32.3 is MANDATORY before every emission. Nothing in this PART
removes or weakens any rule from PARTs 1-31.
